result = process_session(\n   669→            nwb_path,\n   670→            show_processing=args.show_processing and session_count < 2,\n   671→            session_label=session_label\n   672→        )\n   673→\n   674→        if result is not None:\n   675→            all_sessions.append(result)\n   676→            subjects_set.add(result['subject_id'])\n   677→        session_count += 1\n   678→\n   679→    if len(all_sessions) == 0:\n   680→        print(\"No valid sessions found!\")\n   681→        return\n   682→\n   683→    # Build the output data structure\n   684→    subjects = sorted(subjects_set)\n   685→    brain_regions = ['CA1']\n   686→\n   687→    neural_list = []\n   688→    input_list = []\n   689→    output_list = []\n   690→    subject_idx = []\n   691→    brain_region_idx_list = []\n   692→\n   693→    for sess in all_sessions:\n   694→        neural_list.append(sess['neural_trials'])\n   695→        input_list.append(sess['input_trials'])\n   696→        output_list.append(sess['output_trials'])\n   697→        subject_idx.append(subjects.index(sess['subject_id']))\n   698→        brain_region_idx_list.append(np.zeros(sess['n_neurons'], dtype=np.int64))\n   699→\n   700→    # Get effective time bin from first session\n   701→    time_bin_ms = 1000.0 / all_sessions[0]['imaging_rate']\n   702→\n   703→    data = {\n   704→        'neural': neural_list,\n   705→        'input': input_list,\n   706→        'output': output_list,\n   707→\n   708→        'subjects': subjects,\n   709→        'subject_idx': np.array(subject_idx, 'time_from_trial_start_s',\n   716→            'environment_type',\n   717→            'trial_number',\n   718→            'previous_trial_outcome',\n   719→        ],\n   720→        'output_names': [\n   721→            'distance_to_reward_zone',\n   722→            'absolute_position',\n   723→            'speed',\n   724→            'lick',\n   725→            'reward_zone_location',\n   726→            'reward_outcome',\n   727→        ],\n   728→        'output_values': [\n   729→            ['<-50cm', '-50to-10cm', '-10to0cm', '0cm_in_zone', '0to10cm', '10to50cm', '>50cm'],\n   730→            ['0-90cm', '90-180cm', '180-270cm', '270-360cm', '360-450cm'],\n   731→            ['<2cm/s', '2-10cm/s', '10-20cm/s', '20-40cm/s', '>40cm/s'],\n   732→            ['no_lick', 'lick'],\n   733→ train_decoder(neural: list, input: list, output: list, metadata: dict = {}, **kwargs):\n   694→    \"\"\"\n   695→    Trains a common decoder for all sessions to predict output from neural and input data.\n   696→    Jointly learns projection matrices for each session and separate shared decoders for each output dimension.\n   697→\n   698→    Inputs:\n   699→        neural: neural activity. neural is a list of length nsessions, neural[session] is a list\n   700→                of length ntrials[session], and neural[session][trial] is a numpy array of shape (nneurons[session], T[session][trial]),\n   701→                dtype = float32, giving the neural activity for that trial\n   702→        input: variables beyond neural activity the decoder should input, e.g. the stimulis, condition. input is a list of\n   703→                length nsessions, input[session] is a list of length ntrials[session], and input[session][trial] is a numpy array of\n   704→                shape (dinput, T[session][trial]), dtype = float32. with the input variable(s) for each timepoint.\n   705→                dinput is the number of inputs. For discrete inputs, use a one-hot encoding.\n   706→        output: variables we want to decode. output is a list of length nsessions, output[session] is a list of length ntrials[session],\n   707→                and output[session][trial] is a numpy array of shape (doutput, T[session][trial]) of dtype = int with\n   708→                categorical values for each timepoint. Each output[idx, t] is an integer from 0 to ncategories[idx]-1.\n   709→        metadata: optional dict with additional information about the experiment.\n   710→\n   711→        Algorithm hyperparameters can be passed as keyword arguments:\n   712→        npcs: number of projected dimensions to use (default: 10)\n   713→        num_epochs: number of training epochs (default: 200)\n   714→        lr: learning rate (default: 0.001)\n   715→        l1_weight: L1 regularization weight for projection matrices (default: 1e-4)\n   716→        svd_max_samples: maximum number of timepoints to use for SVD initialization (default: 50000).\n   717→                         If a session has more timepoints than this, a random subset will be used.\n   718→        svd_max_neurons: maximum number of neurons to use for SVD initialization (default: 2000).\n   719→                         If a session has more neurons than this, random Gaussian projection will be used.\n   720→                         The final weight is the product of the random projection and SVD matrices.\n   721→        ncategories: optional list of length doutput giving number of categories for each output dimension.\n   722→                     If not provided, will be inferred from data.\n   723→        balanced_loss: if True, use class-weighted cross-entropy loss to compensate for class imbalance.\n   724→                       Weights are inverse of class frequency. Default: False.\n   725→\n   726→    Returns:\n   727→        model: dict with trained decoder parameters. dict with the keys:\n   728→            'projection': list of length nsessions, projection[session] is a torch tensor of shape (npcs, nneurons[session])\n   729→            'decoder': list of length doutput, decoder[out_dim] is a torch linear layer for that output dimension\n   730→            'ncategories': list of length doutput, ncategories[out_dim] is the number of categories for that output\n   731→            'device': torch device used for training\n   732→\n   733→    \"\"\"\n   734→\n   735→    model = {\n   736→        'projection': [],\n   737→        'decoder': [],\n   738→        'ncategories': [],\n   739→        'device': None\n   740→    }\n   741→\n   742→    npcs = kwargs.get('npcs', 10)\n   743→    num_epochs = kwargs.get('num_epochs', 200)\n   744→    lr = kwargs.get('lr', 0.001)\n   745→    l1_weight = kwargs.get('l1_weight', 1e-4)\n   746→    device_str = kwargs.get('device', None)\n   747→    balanced_loss = kwargs.get('balanced_loss', False)\n   748→\n   749→    nsessions = len(neural)\n   750→    if device_str is not None:\n   751→        device = torch.device(device_str)\n   752→    else:\n   753→        device = torch.device('cuda' if torch.cuda.is_available() else 'cpu')\n   754→\n   755→    model['device'] = device\n   756→    print('Using device:', device)\n   757→\n   758→    # Prepare training data using helper function\n   759→    session_data, dinput, doutput = _prepare_session_data(neural, input, output, device)\n   760→\n   761→    # Determine number of categories for each output dimension\n   762→    if 'ncategories' in kwargs:\n   763→        ncategories = kwargs['ncategories']\n   764→    else:\n   765→        ncategories = []\n   766→        for out_dim in range(doutput):\n   767→            max_cat = 0\n   768→            for session in range(nsessions):\n   769→                if session_data[session] is None:\n   770→                    continue\n   771→                max_cat = max(max_cat, int(session_data[session]['output'][:, out_dim].max().item()))\n   772→            ncategories.append(max_cat + 1)\n   773→\n   774→    model['ncategories'] = ncategories\n   775→\n   776→    # Compute class weights (always, for use in chance computation and optionally for balanced loss)\n   777→    class_weights = []\n   778→    class_fractions = []  # Store fractions for chance computation\n   779→    for out_dim in range(doutput):\n   780→        # Count occurrences of each class across all sessions\n   781→        class_counts = np.zeros(ncategories[out_dim])\n   782→        for session in range(nsessions):\n   783→            if session_data[session] is None:\n   784→                continue\n   785→            outputs = session_data[session]['output'][:, out_dim].cpu().numpy()\n   786→            for c in range(ncategories[out_dim]):\n   787→                class_counts[c] += np.sum(outputs == c)\n   788→        # Store class fractions for chance computation\n   789→        total = np.sum(class_counts)\n   790→        fractions = class_counts / total\n   791→        class_fractions.append(fractions)\n   792→        # Compute weights as inverse of frequency (with smoothing to avoid division by zero)\n   793→        weights = total / (ncategories[out_dim] * np.maximum(class_counts, 1))\n   794→        # Normalize weights to sum to 1 (so each output dimension is equally weighted)\n   795→        weights = weights / np.sum(weights)\n   796→        class_weights.append(torch.tensor(weights, dtype=torch.float32, device=device))\n   797→\n   798→    model['class_weights'] = class_weights\n   799→    model['class_fractions'] = class_fractions\n   800→\n   801→    if balanced_loss:\n   802→        print(f\"Using balanced loss with class weights:\")\n   803→        for out_dim in range(doutput):\n   804→            print(f\"  Output {out_dim}: {class_weights[out_dim].cpu().numpy()}\")\n   805→\n   806→    # Initialize projection matrices for each session\n   807→    svd_max_samples = kwargs.get('svd_max_samples', 50000)  # Max timepoints to use for SVD initialization\n   808→    svd_max_neurons = kwargs.get('svd_max_neurons', 2000)   # Max neurons to use for SVD initialization\n   809→    projection_layers = []\n   810→    for session in range(nsessions):\n   811→        nneurons = session_data[session]['nneurons']\n   812→        proj = nn.Linear(nneurons, npcs, bias=False).to(device)\n   813→\n   814→        # Initialize with SVD for stability (use V from SVD of neural data)\n   815→        # For neural data with shape (n_timepoints, n_neurons), SVD gives principal components in V\n   816→        # Use random subsets if data is too large\n   817→        n_timepoints = session_data[session]['neural'].shape[0]\n   818→\n   819→        # Sample timepoints if needed\n   820→        if n_timepoints > svd_max_samples:\n   821→            time_indices = torch.randperm(n_timepoints)[:svd_max_samples]\n   822→            neural_subset = session_data[session]['neural'][time_indices, :]\n   823→            print(f\"Session {session}: Using {svd_max_samples} of {n_timepoints} timepoints for SVD initialization\")\n   824→        else:\n   825→            neural_subset = session_data[session]['neural']\n   826→\n   827→        # Use random projection if too many neurons\n   828→        if nneurons > svd_max_neurons:\n   829→            # Create random Gaussian projection matrix: (n_neurons, svd_max_neurons)\n   830→            P = torch.randn(nneurons, svd_max_neurons)\n   831→            # Project neural data: (n_timepoints, n_neurons) @ (n_neurons, svd_max_neurons) = (n_timepoints, svd_max_neurons)\n   832→            neural_projected = neural_subset @ P\n   833→            print(f\"Session {session}: Using random projection to {svd_max_neurons} of {nneurons} neurons for SVD initialization\")\n   834→\n   835→            # SVD on projected data\n   836→            _, _, V = torch.svd(neural_projected, some=True)\n   837→            # V has shape (svd_max_neurons, min(n_timepoints_subset, svd_max_neurons))\n   838→\n   839→            # Compose: final weight = (P @ V[:, :npcs]).T = V[:, :npcs].T @ P.T\n   840→            n_components = min(npcs, V.shape[1])\n   841→            proj.weight.data[:n_components, :] = (V[:, :n_components].T @ P.T).to(device).clone()\n   842→        else:\n   843→            # Standard SVD without random projection\n   844→            _, _, V = torch.svd(neural_subset, some=True)\n   845→            # V has shape (n_neurons, min(n_timepoints_subset, n_neurons))\n   846→            n_components = min(npcs, V.shape[1])\n   847→            proj.weight.data[:n_components, :] = V[:, :n_components].T.to(device).clone()\n   848→\n   849→        # Initialize remaining components randomly if needed\n   850→        if n_components < npcs:\n   851→            nn.init.orthogonal_(proj.weight.data[n_components:, :])\n   852→\n   853→        projection_layers.append(proj)\n   854→\n   855→    # Initialize separate decoders for each output dimension\n   856→    decoders = []\n   857→    for out_dim in range(doutput):\n   858→        decoder = nn.Linear(npcs + dinput, ncategories[out_dim]).to(device)\n   859→        decoders.append(decoder)\n   860→\n   861→    # Collect all parameters\n   862→    all_params = []\n   863→    for proj in projection_layers:\n   864→        all_params.extend(proj.parameters())\n   865→    for decoder in decoders:\n   866→        all_params.extend(decoder.parameters())\n   867→\n   868→    optimizer = optim.Adam(all_params, lr=lr)\n   869→\n   870→    # Loss function with reduction='none' for manual weighting (matches torch_brain)\n   871→    criterion = nn.CrossEntropyLoss(reduction='none')\n   872→\n   873→    # Training loop\n   874→    # Data stays on CPU; each session is moved to GPU one at a time.\n   875→    # backward() is called per session to free the computation graph before\n   876→    # loading the next session. Gradients accumulate across sessions.\n   877→    for epoch in range(num_epochs):\n   878→        epoch_loss = 0.\n   879→        optimizer.zero_grad()\n   880→\n   881→        for session in range(nsessions):\n   882→            if session_data[session] is None:\n   883→                continue\n   884→            # Move this session's data to device\n   885→            neural_batch = session_data[session]['neural'].to(device)\n   886→            input_batch = session_data[session]['input'].to(device)\n   887→            output_batch = session_data[session]['output'].to(device)\n   888→\n   889→            # Forward pass - shared projection\n   890→            projected = projection_layers[session](neural_batch)\n   891→            combined = torch.cat([projected, input_batch], dim=1)\n   892→\n   893→            # Compute loss for each output dimension\n   894→            session_loss = torch.tensor(0., device=device)\n   895→            for out_dim in range(doutput):\n   896→                logits = decoders[out_dim](combined)\n   897→                target = output_batch[:, out_dim]\n   898→                loss_per_sample = criterion(logits, target)\n   899→\n   900→                if balanced_loss:\n   901→                    # Manual per-sample weighting (matches torch_brain exactly)\n   902→                    sample_weights = class_weights[out_dim][target]\n   903→                    session_loss += (sample_weights * loss_per_sample).sum() / sample_weights.sum()\n   904→                else:\n   905→                    session_loss += loss_per_sample.mean()\n   906→\n   907→            # Add L1 regularization on current session's projection matrix\n   908→            if l1_weight > 0:\n   909→                l1_loss = torch.sum(torch.abs(projection_layers[session].weight))\n   910→                session_loss += l1_weight * l1_loss\n   911→\n   912→            # Backward per session: frees computation graph, accumulates gradients\n   913→            session_loss.backward()\n   914→            epoch_loss += session_loss.item()\n   915→\n   916→        optimizer.step()\n   917→\n   918→        if ((epoch + 1) % 10 == 0) or (epoch < 10):\n   919→            # Loss is sum of per-output-dim means across sessions\n   920→            # Normalize by (nsessions * doutput) to get average per output per session\n   921→            train_loss_normalized = epoch_loss / (nsessions * doutput)\n   922→            print(f'Epoch [{epoch+1}/{num_epochs}], Loss: {train_loss_normalized:.6f}')\n   923→\n   924→    # Store trained parameters\n   925→    for session in range(nsessions):\n   926→        model['projection'].append(projection_layers[session].weight.data.clone())\n   927→    model['decoder'] = decoders\n   928→\n   929→    return model\n   930→\n   931→def predict(neural: list, input: list, model: dict, sessionid: list | None = None, metadata: dict = {}):\n   932→    \"\"\"\n   933→    Uses a trained decoder model to predict output from neural and input data.\n   934→\n   935→    Inputs:\n   936→        neural: neural activity. neural is a list of length nsessions, neural[session] is a list\n   937→                of length ntrials[session], and neural[session][trial] is a numpy array of shape (nneurons[session], T[session][trial]),\n   938→                dtype = float32, giving the neural activity for that trial\n   939→        input: variables beyond neural activity the decoder should input, e.g. the stimulis, condition. input is a list of\n   940→                length nsessions, input[session] is a list of length ntrials[session], and input[session][trial] is a numpy array of\n   941→                shape (dinput, T[session][trial]), dtype = float32. with the input variable(s) for each timepoint.\n   942→                dinput is the number of inputs. Discretized into bins:\n    52→  - 0: < 2 cm/s\n    53→  - 1: 2-10 cm/s\n    54→  - 2: 10-20 cm/s\n    55→  - 3: 20-40 cm/s\n    56→  - 4: > 40 cm/s\n    57→- Lick, time-varying. 0 = no, 1 = yes\n    58→- Reward zone location, per-trial. 0 = A, 1 = B, 2 = C\n    59→- Reward outcome, per-trial. 0 = no, 1 = yes\n    60→\n    61→## Target Data Format\n    62→\n    63→- Save the full converted dataset to the pickle file `converted_data.pkl`.\n    64→- All datasets must be converted into the following Python dictionary structure:\n    65→\n    66→```python\n    67→data = {\n    68→    'neural': [  # list of sessions\n    69→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    70→            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # shape: (n_neurons, n_timepoints)\n    71→            neuron_by_time_matrix,  # trial 2\n    72→            ...\n    73→        ],\n    74→        [  # list of trials for session 2\n    75→            ...\n    76→        ],\n    77→        ...\n    78→    ],\n    79→\n    80→    'input': [  # list of sessions\n    81→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    82→            input_data,  # stimulus/task variables, shape: (n_input, n_timepoints) or (n_input)\n    83→            ...\n    84→        ],\n    85→        ...\n    86→    ],\n    87→\n    88→    'output': [  # list of sessions\n    89→        [  # list of trials for session 1\n    90→            output_data,  # behavioral response, shape (n_output, n_timepoints) or (n_output)\n    91→            ...\n    92→        ],\n    93→        ...\n    94→    ],\n    95→\n    96→    'subjects': list of str, # ids of all subjects, length n_subjects\n    97→    'subject_idx': shape (n_sessions,), # index into subjects for each session\n    98→\n    99→    'brain_regions': list of str, # names of the brain regions recorded from, e.g. ALM, V1\n   100→    'brain_region_idx': [ # list of sessions\n   101→        neuron_region_idx, # shape: (n_neurons,)\n   102→    ] # index into `brain_regions` for each neuron\n   103→\n   104→    'input_names': list of str, # names for each input, of length n_input\n   105→    'output_names': list of str, # names of each output, of length n_output\n   106→    'output_values': [ # list of length n_output\n   107→        [ output_val0, ... ], # list of str of length d_output[0], name of each output value\n   108→        ...\n   109→    ],\n   110→\n   111→    'metadata': {\n   112→        'task_description': str, # description of output tasks\n   113→        'time_bin_size': float, # length of time bin in ms\n   114→        'temporal_alignment_event': str, # description of alignment event\n   115→        'off_start': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to start of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n   116→        'off_end': float or None, # signed time from alignment event to end of trial in seconds, None if N/A\n   117→        ...\n   118→    }\n   119→}\n   120→```\n   121→\n   122→### Key Format Requirements\n   123→\n   124→- **neural**: Neural activity data (e.g., firing rates, spike counts) organized by session and trial\n   125→  - Dimensions: (n_neurons, n_timepoints) for each trial.\n   126→  - Time bins should be the same size for all trials and sessions. \n   127→\n   128→- **input**: Variables that serve as inputs **to the decoder**\n   129→  - These are **decoder inputs**. They could be task inputs to the animal, but inputs to the animal can also be decoder outputs.\n   130→  - Examples: time from an aligning signal, stimulus characteristics such as:\n   131→    - visual: contrast, position, orientation\n   132→    - audio: frequency, amplitude\n   133→  - Dimensions: (d_input, n_timepoints) for each trial. \n   134→  - Should capture all relevant contextual information for the decoder\n   135→  - If an input is a time such as onset of some stimulus, represent it as a binary time series. \n   136→\n   137→- **output**: Variables to be decoded/predicted from neural activity\n   138→  - These are what we want the decoder to **predict**. They could be the animal's output behavior or properties of the stimulus.\n   139→  - Output variables must be **categorical**, i.e. discrete rather than continuous. If a variable is not discrete, follow instructions from Decoder Task to discretize it. \n   140→  - Examples: stimulus properties (contrast, orientation), choice (left/right), reaction time, position\n   141→  - Can be time-varying or discrete values per trial. If at all possible, make it time-varying\n   142→  - If an output is a time such as when a behavior occurred, represent it as a binary time series. \n   143→\n   144→- **subjects**: List of names of all unique subjects (mice).\n   145→- **subject_idx**: Index into subjects for each session.\n   146→  - Order should match order of sessions in `neural`, etc.\n   147→\n   148→- **brain_regions**: List of names of all brain regions recorded from.\n   149→- **brain_region_idx**: Index into brain_regions for each neuron in each session.\n   150→\n   151→- **input_names**: Names of each input variable.\n   152→  - Order should match order of `input` dimensions. \n   153→\n   154→- **output_names**: Names of each output variable.\n   155→  - Order should match order of `output` dimensions.\n   156→- **output_values**: Names of each output value for each output variable.\n   157→  - Order should match categorical `output` values.\n   158→\n   159→- **metadata**: Descriptive information. \n   160→  - `task_description`: Concise description of the task\n   161→  - `temporal_alignment_event`: Concise description of temporal alignment event\n   162→  - `off_start`: Signed time from alignment event to start of trial. Negative means before event, positive after. \n   163→  - `off_end`: Signed time from alignment event to end of trial\n   164→  - Add other relevant fields, e.g. `session_info`\n   165→\n   166→## Python environment\n   167→\n   168→- All required Python packages are already installed in the system Python environment.\n   169→- Use `python3` to run any python code.\n   170→- If you need to install additional libraries, use `pip install`.\n   171→\n   172→## Decoder Reference\n   173→\n   174→- Run this script with `python train_decoder.py <data_file_path>` to validate.\n   175→- Optional arguments:\n   176→  - `--verify-only`: Only verify data format and print summary, then exit (does not train decoder).\n   177→  - `--plot-samples`: Plot sample trials from data and save to png files.\n   178→  - `--cpu`: Force torch to use CPU only (use if GPU runs out of memory).\n   179→- Pipe the output to the designated file so that the user can examine it.\n   180→\n   181→### Purpose\n   182→\n   183→These functions will be used during the validation phase to ensure:\n   184→1. Step 1: Neuropil subtraction\n    F_corr = F_trial - NEUROPIL_COEF * Fneu_trial  # NEUROPIL_COEF = 0.7\n    \n    # Step 2: Maximin baseline per cell\n    # 2a. Gaussian smooth (sigma=15 samples)\n    smoothed = gaussian_filter1d(F_corr, sigma=15, axis=1)\n    # 2b. Minimum filter (morphological operation)\n    baseline = minimum_filter1d(smoothed, size=min(baseline_window, n_t), axis=1)\n    # 2c. Maximum filter (dilation)\n    baseline = maximum_filter1d(baseline, size=min(baseline_window, n_t), axis=1)\n    \n    # Step 3: dF/F = (F - baseline) / |baseline|\n    abs_baseline = np.abs(baseline)\n    abs_baseline[abs_baseline < 1e-10] = 1e-10  # avoid division by zero\n    dff = (F_corr - baseline) / abs_baseline\n    \n    # Step 4: Smooth dF/F (sigma=2 samples)\n    dff = gaussian_filter1d(dff, sigma=DFF_SMOOTH_SIGMA, axis=1)\n    \n    return dff\n```\n\n**Key Parameters:**\n- **Neuropil coefficient**: 0.7\n- **Baseline smoothing sigma**: 15 samples (not time-varying)\n- **Baseline window**: 300 samples (morphological min/max filter size)\n- **Post-dF/F smoothing sigma**: 2 samples\n\n#### **Interneuron Detection** (`detect_interneurons()`, lines 172-209)\n```python\ndef detect_interneurons(dff_all, speed_all, threshold=INTERNEURON_SPEED_CORR_THRESHOLD):\n    \"\"\"\n    Detect putative interneurons by correlation of dF/F with speed.\n    Vectorized implementation.\n    \n    Args:\n        dff_all: (n_cells, n_total_timepoints) - dF/F for all timepoints in session\n        speed_all: (n_total_timepoints,) - speed for all timepoints\n        threshold: correlation threshold (default: 0.5)\n    Returns:\n        is_interneuron: (n_cells,) boolean mask\n    \"\"\"\n    # Only use valid (non-NaN) timepoints\n    valid = ~np.isnan(speed_all) & ~np.isnan(dff_all[0])\n    if valid.sum() < 10:\n        return np.zeros(n_cells, dtype=bool)\n    \n    # Vectorized Pearson correlation\n    speed_valid = speed_all[valid].astype(np.float64)\n    dff_valid = dff_all[:, valid].astype(np.float64)  # (n_cells, n_valid)\n    \n    speed_mean = speed_valid.mean()\n    speed_centered = speed_valid - speed_mean\n    speed_std = np.sqrt(np.sum(speed_centered ** 2))\n    \n    if speed_std < 1e-10:\n        return np.zeros(n_cells, dtype=bool)\n    \n    dff_mean = dff_valid.mean(axis=1, keepdims=True)\n    dff_centered = dff_valid - dff_mean\n    dff_std = np.sqrt(np.sum(dff_centered ** 2, axis=1))\n    \n    # Correlation = dot(dff_centered, speed_centered) / (dff_std * speed_std)\n    corr = dff_centered @ speed_centered / (dff_std * speed_std + 1e-10)\n    \n    is_interneuron = corr > threshold  # default: corr > 0.5\n    \n    return is_interneuron\n```\n\n**Key Step:**\n- Computed on **full session dF/F** and speed data (not per-trial)\n- Uses **Pearson correlation** between neural activity and locomotor speed\n- **Threshold: 0.5** (cells with correlation > 0.5 are excluded)\n\n#### **Cell Filtering Pipeline** (Lines 372-424)\n```python\n# 1. Single location: 'Env1_LocationA', 'Env2_LocationB'\n    # - Switches: 'Env1_LocationB_to_A', 'Env1_LocationA_to_C'\n    # - Training: 'Training...'\n    \n    # Parse scene and assign zones\n    if 'Location' in scene and '_to' not in scene:\n        # Single location throughout session\n        loc = scene.split('Location')[-1]  # 'A', 'B', or 'C'\n        rz_coords[:] = REWARD_ZONE_DICT[loc]\n        rz_labels[:] = loc\n    elif 'A_to' in scene and scene[-1] == 'B':\n        # A -> B switch at default trial 30\n        rz_coords[:30] = REWARD_ZONE_DICT['A']\n        rz_labels[:30] = 'A'\n        rz_coords[30:] = REWARD_ZONE_DICT['B']\n        rz_labels[30:] = 'B'\n    # ... (similar for other switches)\n    \n    return rz_coords, rz_labels\n```\n\n**Fixed Reward Zones** (REWARD_ZONE_DICT):\n- Zone A: [80, 130] cm\n- Zone B: [200, 250] cm\n- Zone C: [320, 370] cm\n- Training: [275, 325] cm\n\n#### **Position Processing** (`discretize_position()`, lines 247-252)\n```python\ndef discretize_position(position, n_bins=POSITION_BINS):\n    \"\"\"Discretize position into equal-sized bins.\"\"\"\n    bin_edges = np.linspace(0, TRACK_LENGTH, n_bins + 1)  # 5 bins over [0, 450]\n    binned = np.digitize(position, bin_edges) - 1\n    binned = np.clip(binned, 0, n_bins - 1)\n    return binned\n```\n**Position Bins:** 5 equal-size bins over 450 cm track (each ~90 cm)\n\n#### **Speed Processing** (`discretize_speed()`, lines 255-269)\n```python\ndef discretize_speed(speed):\n    \"\"\"Discretize speed into 5 bins.\"\"\"\n    out = np.zeros(len(speed), dtype=np.int64)\n    out[speed < 2] = 0      # < 2 cm/s\n    out[(speed >= 2) & (speed < 10)] = 1     # 2-10 cm/s\n    out[(speed >= 10) & (speed < 20)] = 2    # 10-20 cm/s\n    out[(speed >= 20) & (speed < 40)] = 3    # 20-40 cm/s\n    out[speed >= 40] = 4    # > 40 cm/s\n    return out\n```\n**Speed Thresholds:** [0, 2, 10, 20, 40] cm/s\n\n#### **Distance to Reward Zone** (`compute_distance_to_reward_zone()`, lines 212-244)\n```python\ndef compute_distance_to_reward_zone(position, rz_start, rz_end):\n    \"\"\"Compute signed distance from position to nearest point in reward zone.\n    Negative = before reward zone\n    0 = inside\n    Positive = after\n    \"\"\"\n    dist = np.zeros_like(position)\n    before = position < rz_start\n    inside = (position >= rz_start) & (position <= rz_end)\n    after = position > rz_end\n    dist[before] = position[before] - rz_start  # negative\n    dist[inside] = 0.0\n    dist[after] = position[after] - rz_end      # positive\n    return dist\n\ndef discretize_distance_to_rz(dist):\n    \"\"\"Discretize distance to reward zone into 7 bins.\"\"\"\n    out = np.zeros(len(dist), dtype=np.int64)\n    out[dist < -50] = 0              # < -50 cm\n    out[(dist >= -50) & (dist < -10)] = 1      # -50 to -10 cm\n    out[(dist >= -10) & (dist < 0)] = 2        # -10 cm to < 0 cm\n    out[dist == 0] = 3               # 0 cm (inside reward zone)\n    out[(dist > 0) & (dist <= 10)] = 4         # >0 cm to +10 cm\n    out[(dist > 10) & (dist <= 50)] = 5        # +10 to +50 cm\n    out[dist > 50] = 6               # > +50 cm\n    return out\n```\n\n#### **Lick Processing** (Lines 426-465)\n```python\ntrial_lick = lick[s:e].copy()\n\n# Lick sensor error correction\n# if >35% of samples have cumulative lick count > 2, set licks to 0 for this trial\nif np.sum(trial_lick > 2) / n_t > LICK_ERROR_FRACTION_THRESHOLD:  # 0.35\n    trial_lick[:] = 0  # set to 0 instead of NaN for decoder output\n\n# Cap licks at 1 (binary)\ntrial_lick[trial_lick > 1] = 1\ntrial_lick = (trial_lick > 0).astype(np.float32)  # Binary: 0 or 1\n```\n**Lick Error Threshold:** >35% of samples with lick > 2 marks bad trial\n\n#### **Reward Detection** (`detect_reward_in_trial()`, lines 278-282)\n```python\ndef detect_reward_in_trial(reward_timestamps, trial_start_time, trial_end_time):\n    \"\"\"Check if any reward was delivered during the trial.\"\"\"\n    if len(reward_timestamps) == 0:\n        return False\n    return np.any((reward_timestamps >= trial_start_time) & (reward_timestamps <= trial_end_time))\n```\n\n---\n\n### 5. **INPUT & OUTPUT ASSEMBLY** (Lines 472-542)\n\n#### **Input Variables** (Lines 472-505)\n```python\n# Build input array for each trial: shape (4, n_timepoints)\ninput_arr = np.zeros((4, n_t), dtype=np.float32)\n\n# 1. cm\nREWARD_ZONE_DICT = {\n    'A': [80, 130],   # cm\n    'B': [200, 250],\n    'C': [320, 370],\n}\nDEFAULT_CHANGE_TRIAL = 30  # 0-indexed trial where reward zone switches\nINTERNEURON_SPEED_CORR_THRESHOLD = 0.5\nLICK_ERROR_FRACTION_THRESHOLD = 0.35\nNEUROPIL_COEF = 0.7\nDFF_SMOOTH_SIGMA = 2  # samples for post-dF/F Gaussian smoothing\nBASELINE_WINDOW = 300  # samples (~20 sec at 15.5 Hz)\n```\n\n---\n\n### 2. **NEURAL DATA PROCESSING** (Lines 396-424)\n\n#### **dF/F Computation** (`compute_dff_trial()`, lines 136-169)\n```python\ndef compute_dff_trial(F_trial, Fneu_trial, baseline_window=BASELINE_WINDOW):\n    \"\"\"\n    Compute dF/F for a single trial using maximin baseline.\n    Mirrors preprocessing.dff() from reference code.\n    \n    Args:\n        F_trial: (n_cells, n_timepoints) raw fluorescence for one trial\n        Fneu_trial: (n_cells, n_timepoints) neuropil fluorescence\n    Returns:\n        dff: (n_cells, n_timepoints) dF/F\n    \"\"\"\n    # Step 1: Neuropil subtraction\n    F_corr = F_trial - NEUROPIL_COEF * Fneu_trial  # NEUROPIL_COEF = 0.7\n    \n    # Step 2: Maximin baseline per cell\n    # 2a. Gaussian smooth (sigma=15 samples)\n    smoothed = gaussian_filter1d(F_corr, sigma=15, axis=1)\n    # 2b. Minimum filter (morphological operation)\n    baseline = minimum_filter1d(smoothed, size=min(baseline_window, n_t), axis=1)\n    # 2c. Maximum filter (dilation)\n    baseline = maximum_filter1d(baseline, size=min(baseline_window, n_t), axis=1)\n    \n    # Step 3: dF/F = (F - baseline) / |baseline|\n    abs_baseline = np.abs(baseline)\n    abs_baseline[abs_baseline < 1e-10] = 1e-10  # avoid division by zero\n    dff = (F_corr - baseline) / abs_baseline\n    \n    # Step 4: Smooth dF/F (sigma=2 samples)\n    dff = gaussian_filter1d(dff, sigma=DFF_SMOOTH_SIGMA, axis=1)\n    \n    return dff\n```\n\n**Key Parameters:**\n- **Neuropil coefficient**: 0.7\n- **Baseline smoothing sigma**: 15 samples (not time-varying)\n- **Baseline window**: 300 samples (morphological min/max filter size)\n- **Post-dF/F smoothing sigma**: 2 samples\n\n#### **Interneuron Detection** (`detect_interneurons()`, lines 172-209)\n```python\ndef detect_interneurons(dff_all, speed_all, threshold=INTERNEURON_SPEED_CORR_THRESHOLD):\n    \"\"\"\n    Detect putative interneurons by correlation of dF/F with speed.\n    Vectorized implementation.\n    \n    Args:\n        dff_all: (n_cells, n_total_timepoints) - dF/F for all timepoints in session\n        speed_all: (n_total_timepoints,) - speed for all timepoints\n        threshold: correlation threshold (default: 0.5)\n    Returns:\n        is_interneuron: (n_cells,) boolean mask\n    \"\"\"\n    # Only use valid (non-NaN) timepoints\n    valid = ~np.isnan(speed_all) & ~np.isnan(dff_all[0])\n    if valid.sum() < 10:\n        return np.zeros(n_cells, dtype=bool)\n    \n    # Vectorized Pearson correlation\n    speed_valid = speed_all[valid].astype(np.float64)\n    dff_valid = dff_all[:, valid].astype(np.float64)  # (n_cells, n_valid)\n    \n    speed_mean = speed_valid.mean()\n    speed_centered = speed_valid - speed_mean\n    speed_std = np.sqrt(np.sum(speed_centered ** 2))\n    \n    if speed_std < 1e-10:\n        return np.zeros(n_cells, dtype=bool)\n    \n    dff_mean = dff_valid.mean(axis=1, keepdims=True)\n    dff_centered = dff_valid - dff_mean\n    dff_std = np.sqrt(np.sum(dff_centered ** 2, axis=1))\n    \n    # Correlation = dot(dff_centered, speed_centered) / (dff_std * speed_std)\n    corr = dff_centered @ speed_centered / (dff_std * speed_std + 1e-10)\n    \n    is_interneuron = corr > threshold  # default: corr > 0.5\n    \n    return is_interneuron\n```\n\n**Key Step:**\n- Computed on **full session dF/F** and speed data (not per-trial)\n- Uses **Pearson correlation** between neural activity and locomotor speed\n- **Threshold: 0.5** (cells with correlation > 0.5 are excluded)\n\n#### **Cell Filtering Pipeline** (Lines 372-424)\n```python\n# 1. Filter by iscell (from Cellpose/Suite2p)\ncell_mask = iscell[:, 0].astype(bool)\nn_cells_iscell = cell_mask.sum()\n\n# 2. Compute dF/F per trial (on iscell-filtered neurons)\nF_cells = F_all[:, cell_mask].T  # (n_cells, n_timepoints)\nFneu_cells = Fneu_all[:, cell_mask].T\ndff_full = np.full_like(F_cells, np.nan)\nfor i in range(n_trials):\n    s = trial_start_inds[i]\n    e = teleport_inds[i]\n    if e <= s:\n        continue\n    dff_full[:, s:e] = compute_dff_trial(F_cells[:, s:e], Fneu_cells[:, s:e])\n\n# 3. Detect and remove interneurons\nis_interneuron = detect_interneurons(dff_full, speed)\nn_interneurons = is_interneuron.sum()\n\n# 4. Final cell mask: iscell AND not interneuron\nfinal_cell_mask = ~is_interneuron\nn_final_cells = final_cell_mask.sum()\n\n# Use deconvolved events for downstream analysis\nneural_data = deconv_cells[final_cell_mask]  # (n_final_cells, n_timepoints)\n```\n\n**Final Neural Data Used:** Deconvolved events (OASIS) from cells that pass both iscell and interneuron filters\n\n---\n\n### 3. **TRIAL DEFINITION & FILTERING** (Lines 380-391)\n\n```python\n# Find trial boundaries using binary flags\ntrial_start_inds = np.where(trial_start_flag > 0)[0]    # Start of trial (entry to track)\nteleport_inds = np.where(teleport_flag > 0)[0]          # End of trial (teleport back)\n\n# Match trials: must have same number of starts and ends\nn_trials = min(len(trial_start_inds), len(teleport_inds))\n\n# Filter for minimum number of timepoints per trial\nfor i in range(n_trials):\n    s = trial_start_inds[i]\n    e = teleport_inds[i]\n    if e <= s or (e - s) < 2:\n        continue  # Skip short/invalid trials\n```\n\n**Trial Alignment:** \n- **Temporal alignment event**: Start of trial (entry to linear track)\n- **Trial boundaries**: From trial_start_flag to teleport_flag\n\n---\n\n### 4. **BEHAVIORAL VARIABLE PROCESSING** (Lines 426-545)\n\n#### **Reward Zone Determination** (`get_reward_zones()`, lines 70-133)\n```python\ndef get_reward_zones(scene, n_trials, change_trial=DEFAULT_CHANGE_TRIAL):\n    \"\"\"\n    Map scene name to reward zone coordinates per trial.\n    Mirrors behavior.get_reward_zones() from reference code.\n    \n    Returns:\n        rz_coords: (n_trials, 2) array of [start, stop] positions\n        rz_labels: (n_trials,) array of 'A', 'B', or 'C' labels\n    \"\"\"\n    # Examples of scene naming:\n    # - Single location: 'Env1_LocationA', 'Env2_LocationB'\n    # - Switches: 'Env1_LocationB_to_A', 'Env1_LocationA_to_C'\n    # - Training: 'Training...'\n    \n    # Parse scene and assign zones\n    if 'Location' in scene and '_to' not in scene:\n        # Single location throughout session\n        loc = scene.split('Location')[-1]  # 'A', 'B', or 'C'\n        rz_coords[:] = REWARD_ZONE_DICT[loc]\n        rz_labels[:] = loc\n    elif 'A_to' in scene and scene[-1] == 'B':\n        # A -> B switch at default trial 30\n        rz_coords[:30] = REWARD_ZONE_DICT['A']\n        rz_labels[:30] = 'A'\n        rz_coords[30:] = REWARD_ZONE_DICT['B']\n        rz_labels[30:] = 'B'\n    # ... (similar for other switches)\n    \n    return rz_coords, rz_labels\n```\n\n**Fixed Reward Zones** (REWARD_ZONE_DICT):\n- Zone A: [80, 130] cm\n- Zone B: [200, 250] cm\n- Zone C: [320, 370] cm\n- Training: [275, 325] cm\n\n#### **Position Processing** (`discretize_position()`, lines 247-252)\n```python\ndef discretize_position(position, n_bins=POSITION_BINS):\n    \"\"\"Discretize position into equal-sized bins.\"\"\"\n    bin_edges = np.linspace(0, TRACK_LENGTH, n_bins + 1)  # 5 bins over [0, 450]\n    binned = np.digitize(position, bin_edges) - 1\n    binned = np.clip(binned, 0, n_bins - 1)\n    return binned\n```\n**Position Bins:** 5 equal-size bins over 450 cm track (each ~90 cm)\n\n#### **Speed Processing** (`discretize_speed()`, lines 255-269)\n```python\ndef discretize_speed(speed):\n    \"\"\"Discretize speed into 5 bins.\"\"\"\n    out = np.zeros(len(speed), dtype=np.int64)\n    out[speed < 2] = 0      # < 2 cm/s\n    out[(speed >= 2) & (speed < 10)] = 1     # 2-10 cm/s\n    out[(speed >= 10) & (speed < 20)] = 2    # 10-20 cm/s\n    out[(speed >= 20) & (speed < 40)] = 3    # 20-40 cm/s\n    out[speed >= 40] = 4    # > 40 cm/s\n    return out\n```\n**Speed Thresholds:** [0, 2, 10, 20, 40] cm/s\n\n#### **Distance to Reward Zone** (`compute_distance_to_reward_zone()`, lines 212-244)\n```python\ndef compute_distance_to_reward_zone(position, rz_start, rz_end):\n    \"\"\"Compute signed distance from position to nearest point in reward zone.\n    Negative = before reward zone\n    0 = inside\n    Positive = after\n    \"\"\"\n    dist = np.zeros_like(position)\n    before = position < rz_start\n    inside = (position >= rz_start) & (position <= rz_end)\n    after = position > rz_end\n    dist[before] = position[before] - rz_start  # negative\n    dist[inside] = 0.0\n    dist[after] = position[after] - rz_end      # positive\n    return dist\n\ndef discretize_distance_to_rz(dist):\n    \"\"\"Discretize distance to reward zone into 7 bins.\"\"\"\n    out = np.zeros(len(dist), dtype=np.int64)\n    out[dist < -50] = 0              # < -50 cm\n    out[(dist >= -50) & (dist < -10)] = 1      # -50 to -10 cm\n    out[(dist >= -10) & (dist < 0)] = 2        # -10 cm to < 0 cm\n    out[dist == 0] = 3               # 0 cm (inside reward zone)\n    out[(dist > 0) & (dist <= 10)] = 4         # >0 cm to +10 cm\n    out[(dist > 10) & (dist <= 50)] = 5        # +10 to +50 cm\n    out[dist > 50] = 6               # > +50 cm\n    return out\n```\n\n#### **Lick Processing** (Lines 426-465)\n```python\ntrial_lick = lick[s:e].copy()\n\n# Lick sensor error correction\n# if >35% of samples have cumulative lick count > 2, set licks to 0 for this trial\nif np.sum(trial_lick > 2) / n_t > LICK_ERROR_FRACTION_THRESHOLD:  # 0.35\n    trial_lick[:] = 0  # set to 0 instead of NaN for decoder output\n\n# Cap licks at 1 (binary)\ntrial_lick[trial_lick > 1] = 1\ntrial_lick = (trial_lick > 0).astype(np.float32)  # Binary: 0 or 1\n```\n**Lick Error Threshold:** >35% of samples with lick > 2 marks bad trial\n\n#### **Reward Detection** (`detect_reward_in_trial()`, lines 278-282)\n```python\ndef detect_reward_in_trial(reward_timestamps, trial_start_time, trial_end_time):\n    \"\"\"Check if any reward was delivered during the trial.\"\"\"\n    if len(reward_timestamps) == 0:\n        return False\n    return np.any((reward_timestamps >= trial_start_time) & (reward_timestamps <= trial_end_time))\n```\n\n---\n\n### 5. **INPUT & OUTPUT ASSEMBLY** (Lines 472-542)\n\n#### **Input Variables** (Lines 472-505)\n```python\n# Build input array for each trial: shape (4, n_timepoints)\ninput_arr = np.zeros((4, n_t), dtype=np.float32)\n\n# 1. Time from start of trial in seconds (time-varying)\ntime_from_start = (np.arange(n_t) / effective_rate).astype(np.float32)\ninput_arr[0, :] = time_from_start\n\n# 2. Environment type (per-trial, broadcast)\nenv_val = np.median(trial_env[trial_env >= 0]) if np.any(trial_env >= 0) else 0.0\nenv_type = np.float32(env_val)\ninput_arr[1, :] = env_type\n\n# 3. Trial number (per-trial, broadcast)\ntrial_number = np.float32(i)\ninput_arr[2, :] = trial_number\n\n# 4. Previous trial outcome (per-trial, broadcast)\nprev_outcome = np.float32(prev_trial_rewarded)  # 0 or 1\ninput_arr[3, :] = prev_outcome\n```\n\n**Input Names:**\n- `time_from_trial_start_s`\n- `environment_type`\n- `trial_number`\n- `previous_trial_outcome`\n\n#### **Output Variables** (Lines 507-537)\n```python\n# Build output array for each trial: shape (6, n_timepoints)\noutput_arr = np.zeros((6, n_t), dtype=np.int64)\n\n# 1. Distance to reward zone (discretized, time-varying)\nrz_start, rz_end = rz_coords[i]\ndist_to_rz = compute_distance_to_reward_zone(trial_pos, rz_start, rz_end)\ndist_to_rz_binned = discretize_distance_to_rz(dist_to_rz)\noutput_arr[0, :] = dist_to_rz_binned\n\n# 2. Absolute position (discretized, time-varying)\npos_binned = discretize_position(trial_pos)\noutput_arr[1, :] = pos_binned\n\n# 3. Speed (discretized, time-varying)\nspeed_binned = discretize_speed(trial_speed)\noutput_arr[2, :] = speed_binned\n\n# 4. Lick (binary, time-varying)\nlick_binned = trial_lick.astype(np.int64)\noutput_arr[3, :] = lick_binned\n\n# 5. Reward zone location (per-trial, broadcast)\nrz_loc = rz_label_to_idx(rz_labels[i])  # 0=A, 1=B, 2=C\noutput_arr[4, :] = rz_loc\n\n# 6. Reward outcome (per-trial, broadcast)\nreward_outcome = int(was_rewarded)  # 0 or 1\noutput_arr[5, :] = reward_outcome\n```\n\n**Output Names & Value Categories:**\n1. `distance_to_reward_zone`: `['<-50cm', '-50to-10cm', '-10to0cm', '0cm_in_zone', '0to10cm', '10to50cm', '>50cm']`\n2. `absolute_position`: `['0-90cm', '90-180cm', '180-270cm', '270-360cm', '360-450cm']`\n3. `speed`: `['<2cm/s', '2-10cm/s', '10-20cm/s', '20-40cm/s', '>40cm/s']`\n4. `lick`: `['no_lick', 'lick']`\n5. `reward_zone_location`: `['zone_A', 'zone_B', 'zone_C']`\n6. `reward_outcome`: `['no_reward', 'reward']`\n\n---\n\n### 6. **DATA STRUCTURE OUTPUT** (Lines 703-755)\n\n**Final pickle format saved to `converted_data.pkl`:**\n\n```python\ndata = {\n    # Neural activity (deconvolved events, post-interneuron filtering)\n    'neural': [session_list_of_trials],  # list of sessions\n    \n    # Decoder inputs\n    'input': [session_list_of_trials],\n    \n    # Decoder outputs (categorical)\n    'output': [session_list_of_trials],\n    \n    # Subject and session metadata\n    'subjects': list_of_subject_ids,\n    'subject_idx': np.array([subject_index_per_session]),\n    \n    # Brain region info\n    'brain_regions': ['CA1'],\n    'brain_region_idx': [np.array([region_indices_per_neuron])],\n    \n    # Variable names\n    'input_names': ['time_from_trial_start_s', 'environment_type', \n                    'trial_number', 'previous_trial_outcome'],\n    'output_names': ['distance_to_reward_zone', 'absolute_position', \n                     'speed', 'lick', The AI tracks the reward outcome of the previous trial via `prev_trial_rewarded`, initialized to 0 for the first trial. Updated at the end of each trial loop iteration.\n\n## 6-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Previous trial outcome*?\n\ni. For each trial, reward is detected by checking if any entry in `reward_timestamps` falls within the trial's time window `[trial_start_time, trial_end_time]`. The previous trial's reward outcome (0=omitted, 1=rewarded) is stored and used as input for the current trial. The first trial defaults to 0 (no previous reward).\n\nii.\n```python\nprev_trial_rewarded = 0  # For the first trial, assume no previous reward\n# ...\nfor i in range(n_trials):\n    # ... at end of loop:\n    prev_trial_rewarded = int(was_rewarded)\n```\n\niii. The AI correctly tracks reward state across trials. Skipped trials also update `prev_trial_rewarded`.\n\n## 7-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Distance to reward zone* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from: (1) `position` behavioral timeseries, and (2) reward zone coordinates determined from the session's scene name (parsed from the NWB `identifier` field) using the `REWARD_ZONE_DICT`.\n\nii.\n```python\ntrial_pos = position[s:e]\nrz_start, rz_end = rz_coords[i]\ndist_to_rz = compute_distance_to_reward_zone(trial_pos, rz_start, rz_end)\n```\n\niii. The AI mapped: \"Distance to reward zone | output[0] | Discretized into 7 bins, time-varying | Compute from position and reward zone coords.\"\n\n## 7-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Distance to reward zone*?\n\ni. Signed distance from position to the nearest edge of the reward zone: negative if before the zone, 0 if inside, positive if after.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef compute_distance_to_reward_zone(position, rz_start, rz_end):\n    dist = np.zeros_like(position)\n    before = position < rz_start\n    inside = (position >= rz_start) & (position <= rz_end)\n    after = position > rz_end\n    dist[before] = position[before] - rz_start  # negative\n    dist[inside] = 0.0\n    dist[after] = position[after] - rz_end  # positive\n    return dist\n```\n\niii. This matches the concept of \"distance to any location in the reward zone\" from the instructions.\n\n## 7-c. How is `output` *Distance to reward zone* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. Discretized into 7 bins as specified in the instructions:\n- 0: < -50 cm\n- 1: -50 to -10 cm\n- 2: -10 cm to < 0 cm\n- 3: 0 cm (inside reward zone)\n- 4: >0 cm to +10 cm\n- 5: +10 to +50 cm\n- 6: > +50 cm\n\nii.\n```python\ndef discretize_distance_to_rz(dist):\n    out = np.zeros(len(dist), dtype=np.int64)\n    out[dist < -50] = 0\n    out[(dist >= -50) & (dist < -10)] = 1\n    out[(dist >= -10) & (dist < 0)] = 2\n    out[dist == 0] = 3\n    out[(dist > 0) & (dist <= 10)] = 4\n    out[(dist > 10) & (dist <= 50)] = 5\n    out[dist > 50] = 6\n    return out\n```\n\niii. Exactly matches the bin specifications in the instructions.\n\n## 7-d. How is `output` *Distance to reward zone* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. The distance is computed from the position values at the same trial indices (s:e) as the neural data, ensuring frame-by-frame alignment.\n\nii.\n```python\ntrial_pos = position[s:e]\n# ...\noutput_arr[0, :] = dist_to_rz_binned  # same n_t as neural\n```\n\niii. Derived from the `position` behavioral timeseries at `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/position/data`.\n\nii.\n```python\nposition = bts['position/data'][:]\n# ...\ntrial_pos = position[s:e]\npos_binned = discretize_position(trial_pos)\n```\n\niii. The raw position values (in cm, range [0, 450]) are discretized into 5 equal-sized bins using `np.digitize` with bin edges at [0, 90, 180, 270, 360, 450].\n\nii.\n```python\ndef discretize_position(position, n_bins=POSITION_BINS):\n    bin_edges = np.linspace(0, TRACK_LENGTH, n_bins + 1)\n    binned = np.digitize(position, bin_edges) - 1\n    binned = np.clip(binned, 0, n_bins - 1)\n    return binned\n```\n\niii. The instructions specify \"Absolute position in corridor, discretized into 5 equal-sized bins, time-varying.\"\n\n## 8-c. How is `output` *Absolute position* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. 5 equal bins of 90 cm each:\n- 0: 0-90 cm\n- 1: 90-180 cm\n- 2: 180-270 cm\n- 3: 270-360 cm\n- 4: 360-450 cm\n\nii.\n```python\nPOSITION_BINS = 5\nPOSITION_BIN_EDGES = np.linspace(0, TRACK_LENGTH, POSITION_BINS + 1)  # [0, 90, 180, 270, 360, 450]\n```\n\niii. A simple computation: create integer indices 0 to n_t-1, divide by the effective imaging rate (Hz) to convert to seconds.\n\nii.\n```python\ntime_from_start = (np.arange(n_t) / effective_rate).astype(np.float32)\n```\n\niii. No complex processing needed. The AI uses frame indices and the known imaging rate for conversion.\n\n## 3-c. How is the `input` *Time from start of trial in seconds* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. Both neural data and time are indexed from the same trial start index `s` to teleport index `e`. The time array has the same number of elements (`n_t = e - s`) as neural timepoints, ensuring 1:1 alignment.\n\nii.\n```python\nn_t = e - s\ntime_from_start = (np.arange(n_t) / effective_rate).astype(np.float32)\ninput_arr[0, :] = time_from_start\n# neural: trial_neural = neural_data[:, s:e]  # same n_t timepoints\n```\n\niii. Alignment is inherent because both use the same trial window indices.\n\n## 4-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Environment type* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from the `environment` behavioral timeseries in the NWB file at `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/environment/data`.\n\nii.\n```python\nenvironment = bts['environment/data'][:]\n# ...\ntrial_env = environment[s:e]\nenv_val = np.median(trial_env[trial_env >= 0]) if np.any(trial_env >= 0) else 0.0\nenv_type = np.float32(env_val)\n```\n\niii. The AI mapped: \"Environment (morph) | input[1] | 0=ENV1, 1=ENV2, per-trial scalar | From `environment` behavioral TS\".\n\n## 4-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Environment type*?\n\ni. For each trial, the environment values within the trial window are extracted. Negative values are excluded, and the median of the remaining values is taken as the per-trial environment type. This is then broadcast across all timepoints in the trial.\n\nii.\n```python\nenv_val = np.median(trial_env[trial_env >= 0]) if np.any(trial_env >= 0) else 0.0\nenv_type = np.float32(env_val)\ninput_arr[1, :] = env_type  # broadcast\n```\n\niii. Using median of non-negative values is a robust way to get the per-trial environment type value (0 or 1).\n\n## 5-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Trial number* derived from?\n\ni. The trial number is derived from the loop index `i` (0-indexed) over trials within each session, NOT from the `trial number` behavioral timeseries in the NWB file.\n\nii.\n```python\ntrial_number = np.float32(i)\ninput_arr[2, :] = trial_number  # broadcast\n```\n\niii. The AI uses the loop iteration counter as trial number, which represents the 0-indexed trial position within the session.\n\n## 5-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Trial number*?\n\ni. The trial number is simply the 0-indexed loop variable `i`, cast to float32 and broadcast across all timepoints in the trial.\n\nii.\n```python\ntrial_number = np.float32(i)\ninput_arr[2, :] = trial_number  # broadcast\n```\n\niii. No further processing. This counts all trials including those that get skipped (invalid trials increment `i` but don't produce output), so the trial numbers may have gaps.\n\n## 6-a. What variables in the raw data is `input` *Previous trial outcome* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from the `Reward/timestamps` behavioral timeseries (reward delivery timestamps) by checking whether any reward timestamp falls within the previous trial's time window.\n\nii.\n```python\nreward_timestamps = bts['Reward/timestamps'][:]\n# ...\ndef detect_reward_in_trial(reward_timestamps, trial_start_time, trial_end_time):\n    if len(reward_timestamps) == 0:\n        return False\n    return np.any((reward_timestamps >= trial_start_time) & (reward_timestamps <= trial_end_time))\n# ...\nprev_trial_rewarded = int(was_rewarded)\nprev_outcome = np.float32(prev_trial_rewarded)\ninput_arr[3, :] = prev_outcome  # broadcast\n```\n\niii. The AI tracks the reward outcome of the previous trial via `prev_trial_rewarded`, initialized to 0 for the first trial. Updated at the end of each trial loop iteration.\n\n## 6-b. What processing is involved in computing `input` *Previous trial outcome*?\n\ni. For each trial, reward is detected by checking if any entry in `reward_timestamps` falls within the trial's time window `[trial_start_time, trial_end_time]`. The previous trial's reward outcome (0=omitted, 1=rewarded) is stored and used as input for the current trial. The first trial defaults to 0 (no previous reward).\n\nii.\n```python\nprev_trial_rewarded = 0  # For the first trial, assume no previous reward\n# ...\nfor i in range(n_trials):\n    # ... at end of loop:\n    prev_trial_rewarded = int(was_rewarded)\n```\n\niii. The AI correctly tracks reward state across trials. Skipped trials also update `prev_trial_rewarded`.\n\n## 7-a. What variables in the raw data is `output` *Distance to reward zone* derived from?\n\ni. Derived from: (1) `position` behavioral timeseries, and (2) reward zone coordinates determined from the session's scene name (parsed from the NWB `identifier` field) using the `REWARD_ZONE_DICT`.\n\nii.\n```python\ntrial_pos = position[s:e]\nrz_start, rz_end = rz_coords[i]\ndist_to_rz = compute_distance_to_reward_zone(trial_pos, rz_start, rz_end)\n```\n\niii. The AI mapped: \"Distance to reward zone | output[0] | Discretized into 7 bins, time-varying | Compute from position and reward zone coords.\"\n\n## 7-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Distance to reward zone*?\n\ni. Signed distance from position to the nearest edge of the reward zone: negative if before the zone, 0 if inside, positive if after.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef compute_distance_to_reward_zone(position, rz_start, rz_end):\n    dist = np.zeros_like(position)\n    before = position < rz_start\n    inside = (position >= rz_start) & (position <= rz_end)\n    after = position > rz_end\n    dist[before] = position[before] - rz_start  # negative\n    dist[inside] = 0.0\n    dist[after] = position[after] - rz_end  # positive\n    return dist\n```\n\niii. This matches the concept of \"distance to any location in the reward zone\" from the instructions.\n\n## 7-c. How is `output` *Distance to reward zone* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. Discretized into 7 bins as specified in the instructions:\n- 0: < -50 cm\n- 1: -50 to -10 cm\n- 2: -10 cm to < 0 cm\n- 3: 0 cm (inside reward zone)\n- 4: >0 cm to +10 cm\n- 5: +10 to +50 cm\n- 6: > +50 cm\n\nii.\n```python\ndef discretize_distance_to_rz(dist):\n    out = np.zeros(len(dist), dtype=np.int64)\n    out[dist < -50] = 0\n    out[(dist >= -50) & (dist < -10)] = 1\n    out[(dist >= -10) & (dist < 0)] = 2\n    out[dist == 0] = 3\n    out[(dist > 0) & (dist <= 10)] = 4\n    out[(dist > 10) & (dist <= 50)] = 5\n    out[dist > 50] = 6\n    return out\n```\n\niii. Exactly matches the bin specifications in the instructions.\n\n## 7-d. How is `output` *Distance to reward zone* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. The distance is computed from the position values at the same trial indices (s:e) as the neural data, ensuring frame-by-frame alignment.\n\nii.\n```python\ntrial_pos = position[s:e]\n# ...\noutput_arr[0, :] = dist_to_rz_binned  # same n_t as neural\n```\n\niii. Derived from the `position` behavioral timeseries at `processing/behavior/BehavioralTimeSeries/position/data`.\n\nii.\n```python\nposition = bts['position/data'][:]\n# ...\ntrial_pos = position[s:e]\npos_binned = discretize_position(trial_pos)\n```\n\niii. Direct use of the position variable from the NWB file.\n\n## 8-b. What processing is involved in computing `output` *Absolute position*?\n\ni. The raw position values (in cm, range [0, 450]) are discretized into 5 equal-sized bins using `np.digitize` with bin edges at [0, 90, 180, 270, 360, 450].\n\nii.\n```python\ndef discretize_position(position, n_bins=POSITION_BINS):\n    bin_edges = np.linspace(0, TRACK_LENGTH, n_bins + 1)\n    binned = np.digitize(position, bin_edges) - 1\n    binned = np.clip(binned, 0, n_bins - 1)\n    return binned\n```\n\niii. The instructions specify \"Absolute position in corridor, discretized into 5 equal-sized bins, time-varying.\"\n\n## 8-c. How is `output` *Absolute position* thresholded into categories?\n\ni. 5 equal bins of 90 cm each:\n- 0: 0-90 cm\n- 1: 90-180 cm\n- 2: 180-270 cm\n- 3: 270-360 cm\n- 4: 360-450 cm\n\nii.\n```python\nPOSITION_BINS = 5\nPOSITION_BIN_EDGES = np.linspace(0, TRACK_LENGTH, POSITION_BINS + 1)  # [0, 90, 180, 270, 360, 450]\n```\n\niii. Matches the instruction to use \"5 equal-sized bins\" over the 450 cm track.\n\n## 8-d. How is `output` *Absolute position* aligned with the neural data?\n\ni. Two-step processing: (1) Lick sensor error correction - if >35% of samples in a trial have cumulative lick count > 2, set all licks in that trial to 0. (2) Cap all lick values at 1 to produce a binary (0/1) output.\n\nii.\n```python\n# Lick sensor error correction\nif np.sum(trial_lick > 2) / n_t > LICK_ERROR_FRACTION_THRESHOLD:\n    trial_lick[:] = 0  # set to 0 instead of NaN for decoder output\n# Cap licks at 1 (binary)\ntrial_lick[trial_lick > 1] = 1\ntrial_lick = (trial_lick > 0).astype(np.float32)\n```\n\niii. For single-location sessions, all trials get the same zone.\n\nii.\n```python\ndef get_reward_zones(scene, n_trials, change_trial=DEFAULT_CHANGE_TRIAL):\n    if 'Location' in scene and '_to' not in scene:\n        loc = scene.split('Location')[-1]\n        rz_coords[:] = REWARD_ZONE_DICT[loc]\n        rz_labels[:] = loc\n    elif 'A_to' in scene and scene[-1] == 'B':\n        rz_coords[:change_trial] = REWARD_ZONE_DICT['A']\n        rz_labels[:change_trial] = 'A'\n        rz_coords[change_trial:] = REWARD_ZONE_DICT['B']\n        rz_labels[change_trial:] = 'B'\n    # ... similar for other switch combinations\n```\n\niii.
